Many factors, including the health of the individual, the transient nature of some pathogens, and the presence and expression of virulence factors all contribute to an individual's symptoms.\u003cbr\u003e\u003cbr\u003eToxins are a type of virulence factor produced by certain pathogens. Many individuals come into contact with opportunistic bacteria and experience no symptoms. Most sources consider these microbes to be normal in the stool. However, they can cause gastroenteritis and inflammation at high levels in vulnerable patients. Symptoms may include diarrhea, loose stools, abdominal pain, or even constipation.\u003cbr\u003e\u003cbr\u003eOvergrowth and excessive colonization by opportunistic bacteria may occur when the commensal bacteria are impaired by poor diet, antibiotic use, parasitic infection, or a weakened immune system. In a patient with suspected fungal overgrowth, additional tests may be necessary to understand the complete picture of fungal overgrowth.
